Seeing the pictures of the old machinery there... well, it just sets me off, so I stay away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Are you unusually jumpy or easily startled since the accident?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really am. I'm just constantly on edge. Even when I'm somewhere quiet, like sitting in the break room, or... or at rehearsal, I’m always watching the doors. I catch myself listening for sudden machine noises all the time, and I'm constantly checking to see who’s moving behind me. It's just like my body won’t believe that I’m safe anymore.\n\nDoctor: During the machinery accident, did you see someone get seriously injured or killed?\n\nPatient: No, I... I didn't see anyone get hurt like that. My coworker was right there, but... no, thankfully, nobody was seriously injured or killed. It was just the... the machine itself failing so violently.\n\nDoctor: At the time of the machinery accident, did you think that you or someone else might be killed or seriously injured?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't think anyone was going to die or get hurt like that. It was... it was loud and terrifying when the machine broke apart, but I didn't actually think my life was in danger in that moment. I just wanted to get away from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about everyday things, even when they’re not related to the machinery accident?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about normal things like bills or getting to work on time, but it's not... it's not a lot. It's just the normal amount of worrying, I think. My mind is mostly just stuck on the accident and feeling like something bad is about to happen right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Now the younger inspectors at work act like they're scared to even ask me a simple question. I just can't seem to control my temper lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you felt unusually energized with much less need for sleep?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I mean, I definitely don't feel energized. I never have times where I feel like I just don't need sleep.\n\nDoctor: When you snap at people, does it usually feel like your reaction is much bigger than what set you off?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely does. Like I was saying about that safety meeting... whatever we were talking about wasn't even a big deal, but I just exploded. It's way out of proportion. That's exactly why those younger inspectors are so scared to ask me anything now. I just go off on them over the smallest things, and I don't even realize how bad my reaction is until I'm already yelling.\n\nDoctor: When you blow up at people, are you doing it on purpose to get something you want?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I'm not doing it on purpose at all. I don't... I don't want anything from them when I get like that. It's just a reaction that comes out before I can stop it. If anything, I hate that I do it, because I know I'm just pushing people away, not trying to get my way.\n\nDoctor: After one of these outbursts, do you feel genuinely sorry for what you did?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I'm not really sure if I feel sorry, exactly. I mean, I know I hate that I do it, like I just said, but... I don't know. I haven't really stopped to think about whether I feel genuinely sorry afterward. I just kind of know I messed up again and leave it at that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I mean, I get frustrated with myself and how things are going at work, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling down or sad most days. That hasn't really been a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do your close relationships tend to swing between getting very intense and then falling apart?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my relationships don't really swing back and forth like that. I've been married for a long time, and... well, I guess things are just pretty steady. The only real issue is me snapping at my husband sometimes, but it doesn't mean things fall apart or get super intense. It's just... normal, I guess, besides my outbursts.\n\nDoctor: Did these angry outbursts start after a major stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. This whole thing started after a machinery accident at work. They had just switched us to an overnight schedule, and... well, that's when the accident happened. It's been going on ever since then, which is about two years and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have unwanted memories of that machinery accident that keep coming back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It doesn't take much to bring them on, either. Just hearing a loud clank from a cart, or... or smelling hot oil... it puts me right back in that bay. I just see my coworker right there by the machine, and it's like it's happening right in front of me all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you go out of your way to avoid reminders of the machinery accident?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. At work, I go out of my way so I don't have to pass the inspection bay. And I won't talk about the accident if I can help it... I just refuse to. I even quit going to these industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. Seeing the pictures of the old machinery there... well, it just sets me off, so I stay away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Are you unusually jumpy or easily startled since the accident?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really am. I'm just constantly on edge. Even when I'm somewhere quiet, like sitting in the break room, or... or at rehearsal, I’m always watching the doors. I catch myself listening for sudden machine noises all the time, and I'm constantly checking to see who’s moving behind me. It's just like my body won’t believe that I’m safe anymore.\n\nDoctor: During the machinery accident, did you see someone get seriously injured or killed?\n\nPatient: No, I... Like I was saying about that safety meeting... whatever we were talking about wasn't even a big deal, but I just exploded. It's way out of proportion. That's exactly why those younger inspectors are so scared to ask me anything now. I just go off on them over the smallest things, and I don't even realize how bad my reaction is until I'm already yelling.\n\nDoctor: When you blow up at people, are you doing it on purpose to get something you want?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I'm not doing it on purpose at all. I don't... I don't want anything from them when I get like that. It's just a reaction that comes out before I can stop it. If anything, I hate that I do it, because I know I'm just pushing people away, not trying to get my way.\n\nDoctor: After one of these outbursts, do you feel genuinely sorry for what you did?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I'm not really sure if I feel sorry, exactly. I mean, I know I hate that I do it, like I just said, but... I don't know. I haven't really stopped to think about whether I feel genuinely sorry afterward. I just kind of know I messed up again and leave it at that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I wouldn't say I feel depressed. I mean, I get frustrated with myself and how things are going at work, but... no, I don't think I've been feeling down or sad most days. That hasn't really been a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do your close relationships tend to swing between getting very intense and then falling apart?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, my relationships don't really swing back and forth like that. I've been married for a long time, and... well, I guess things are just pretty steady. The only real issue is me snapping at my husband sometimes, but it doesn't mean things fall apart or get super intense. It's just... normal, I guess, besides my outbursts.\n\nDoctor: Did these angry outbursts start after a major stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually. This whole thing started after a machinery accident at work. They had just switched us to an overnight schedule, and... well, that's when the accident happened. It's been going on ever since then, which is about two years and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have unwanted memories of that machinery accident that keep coming back?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It doesn't take much to bring them on, either. Just hearing a loud clank from a cart, or... or smelling hot oil... it puts me right back in that bay. I just see my coworker right there by the machine, and it's like it's happening right in front of me all over again.\n\nDoctor: Do you go out of your way to avoid reminders of the machinery accident?\n\nPatient: Yes, I definitely do. At work, I go out of my way so I don't have to pass the inspection bay. And I won't talk about the accident if I can help it... I just refuse to. I even quit going to these industrial-history talks that I used to really enjoy. Seeing the pictures of the old machinery there... well, it just sets me off, so I stay away from all of it.\n\nDoctor: Are you unusually jumpy or easily startled since the accident?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really am. I'm just constantly on edge. Even when I'm somewhere quiet, like sitting in the break room, or... or at rehearsal, I’m always watching the doors. I catch myself listening for sudden machine noises all the time, and I'm constantly checking to see who’s moving behind me. It's just like my body won’t believe that I’m safe anymore.\n\nDoctor: During the machinery accident, did you see someone get seriously injured or killed?\n\nPatient: No, I... I didn't see anyone get hurt like that. My coworker was right there, but... no, thankfully, nobody was seriously injured or killed. It was just the... the machine itself failing so violently.\n\nDoctor: At the time of the machinery accident, did you think that you or someone else might be killed or seriously injured?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't think anyone was going to die or get hurt like that. It was... it was loud and terrifying when the machine broke apart, but I didn't actually think my life was in danger in that moment. I just wanted to get away from it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about everyday things, even when they’re not related to the machinery accident?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I worry about normal things like bills or getting to work on time, but it's not... it's not a lot. It's just the normal amount of worrying, I think. My mind is mostly just stuck on the accident and feeling like something bad is about to happen right now.\n\nDoctor: When the machine failed, did you believe you were in immediate physical danger?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. I mean, it was scary because it was so sudden and loud, but I didn't think it was going to hit me or anything. I just knew I needed to step back. I didn't feel like I was in immediate danger of getting hurt.\n\nDoctor: Before the machinery accident, did you already have episodes where you would suddenly explode in anger like this?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't. I mean, I might get frustrated sometimes, like anyone else, but I never had these... these sudden outbursts where I just lose my temper completely. That's all new since the accident.\n\nDoctor: How often do these angry outbursts happen?\n\nPatient: It's happening a lot. I just... I keep snapping at people over little things, and it's actually why I'm here. Like at a recent safety meeting, I just started yelling before I could even stop myself. It's gotten to the point where the younger inspectors act scared to even ask me anything on the floor.\n\nDoctor: About how often do you have these sudden angry outbursts?\n\nPatient: Well, like I said, it's happening a lot. I'd say... I don't know exactly, maybe a few times a week? It just feels like it's all the time lately.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
